Theorem 215.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The spectral operator L associated with convergence analysis theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 660. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 167 with 3 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: Consider the convergence analysis operator L on the spectral space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 660 for all k. By the convergence analysis theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 167. QED.

Theorem 215.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on spectral structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 112. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 241 with relations in degree 4.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 112 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the spectral structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 241. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 4. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 215.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: Given a spectral algebra A over field F with invariance principle conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 698. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 536/5.
